Appendix B — Full-Length Practice Exam

30 original questions, timed at 3 hours (10 minutes per 6 questions is a good pacing checkpoint), mirroring the real distribution of topics across General Probability, Univariate Random Variables, and Multivariate Random Variables. Every question below is original to this manual.

Cover the answer choices with a sheet of paper if you can, solve first, then check. Flag any question that takes longer than 6 minutes and return to it at the end.

Practice Exam — 30 Questions

1. Let p(n) denote the probability that a claims adjuster closes exactly n files in a day, and suppose p(n+1) = 0.35·p(n) for all integers n ≥ 0. Calculate the probability that the adjuster closes more than 2 files in a day.
(A) 0.0150  (B) 0.0429  (C) 0.0613  (D) 0.0875  (E) 0.1225
2. Three underwriters review applications. Let A = referred to a senior underwriter, B = flagged for fraud review, C = requires additional documentation. For a batch of applications: P(A)=0.35, P(B)=0.25, P(C)=0.40, P(A∩B)=0.12, P(A∩C)=0.15, P(B∩C)=0.10, P(A∩B∩C)=0.05. Calculate the probability that none of the three occur.
(A) 0.22  (B) 0.28  (C) 0.32  (D) 0.38  (E) 0.44
3. Losses (before any deductible) for a peril are Exponential with mean 8 (in thousands). A policy has an ordinary deductible of 3 (in thousands). Calculate the expected payment per loss, net of the deductible.
(A) 3.38  (B) 4.62  (C) 5.50  (D) 6.25  (E) 7.02
4. Let X and Y have joint pmf given by p(0,0)=0.10, p(0,1)=0.15, p(1,0)=0.20, p(1,1)=0.35, p(2,0)=0.05, p(2,1)=0.15 (for X=0,1,2 and Y=0,1). Given Y=1, calculate Var(X|Y=1).
(A) 0.231  (B) 0.346  (C) 0.462  (D) 0.577  (E) 0.692
5. The number of customer complaints received by a call center in an hour is Poisson with mean 3.5. Calculate the probability of at least 2 complaints in a given hour.
(A) 0.678  (B) 0.746  (C) 0.803  (D) 0.864  (E) 0.917
6. A pension administrator estimates that 40% of participants who have only a 401(k) will roll over their balance, and 60% of participants who have only an IRA will roll over. 80% of participants who have both a 401(k) and an IRA will roll over at least one account. Records show 70% of participants have a 401(k), 50% have an IRA, and 20% have both. Calculate the percentage of participants who will roll over at least one account.
(A) 34%  (B) 42%  (C) 48%  (D) 54%  (E) 61%
7. A committee of 4 is chosen at random from a pool of 7 actuaries and 5 underwriters (12 people total). Calculate the probability the committee has exactly 2 actuaries and 2 underwriters.
(A) 0.152  (B) 0.303  (C) 0.354  (D) 0.424  (E) 0.485
8. Compare the standard deviation of a discrete uniform distribution on {1,2,…,20} to that of a continuous uniform distribution on [1,20]. Calculate the ratio (discrete SD) / (continuous SD).
(A) 0.951  (B) 1.000  (C) 1.051  (D) 1.098  (E) 1.147
9. Let C and D be the number of claims from two independent adjusters' territories in a week. Given C+D=3, the conditional distribution of C is: P(C=0|·)=0.20, P(C=1|·)=0.30, P(C=2|·)=0.35, P(C=3|·)=0.15. Calculate Var(C | C+D=3).
(A) 0.712  (B) 0.828  (C) 0.948  (D) 1.062  (E) 1.185
10. For an Exponential random variable with mean μ, calculate the ratio of its median to its mean.
(A) 0.500  (B) 0.693  (C) 0.786  (D) 0.900  (E) 1.000
11. Annual claim severities for a book of 8 independent policies are i.i.d. with mean 600 and variance 360,000 each. Let S be the total severity across all 8 policies. Calculate Var(S).
(A) 1,440,000  (B) 2,160,000  (C) 2,880,000  (D) 3,600,000  (E) 4,320,000
12. A factory experiences equipment breakdowns following a Poisson process averaging 6 breakdowns per 3-year period, with i.i.d. Exponential times between breakdowns. Calculate the median time (in years) until the next breakdown.
(A) 0.203  (B) 0.288  (C) 0.347  (D) 0.416  (E) 0.500
13. A claims office receives applications from three regions: North (30% of applications), Central (45%), South (25%). Historically 4% of North applications, 7% of Central applications, and 10% of South applications are fraudulent. An application is selected at random and found to be fraudulent. Calculate the probability it came from the Central region.
(A) 0.175  (B) 0.365  (C) 0.460  (D) 0.535  (E) 0.640
14. Losses are uniformly distributed on [0,200]. A deductible d is chosen so that the expected claim payment (net of the deductible) equals 40. Calculate d.
(A) 42.3  (B) 58.6  (C) 73.5  (D) 91.7  (E) 110.2
15. Suppose Var(X)=25, Var(Y)=16, Cov(X,Y)=6. Calculate Var(3X − 2Y + 10).
(A) 145  (B) 181  (C) 217  (D) 253  (E) 289
16. A quality inspector tests items from a production line; each item independently fails inspection with probability 0.30. Calculate the probability that at least 3 of the first 5 items tested fail inspection.
(A) 0.031  (B) 0.087  (C) 0.163  (D) 0.247  (E) 0.360
17. X and Y are independent with Var(X)=7 and Var(Y)=12. Let W = 5X − 3Y. Calculate Var(W).
(A) 175  (B) 217  (C) 250  (D) 283  (E) 316
18. Losses follow an Exponential distribution with mean 12. A policy has an ordinary deductible of 5. Calculate the expected amount retained by the policyholder (i.e., the expected unreimbursed loss) per loss.
(A) 2.85  (B) 3.47  (C) 4.09  (D) 4.68  (E) 5.21
19. For a portfolio, the probability of a wind claim in a year is 15% and the probability of a flood claim is 25%, independently. Calculate the probability of exactly one of the two claim types (not both).
(A) 0.185  (B) 0.260  (C) 0.325  (D) 0.375  (E) 0.400
20. Let X1,…,X4 be i.i.d. Uniform(0,10). Calculate E[max(X1,…,X4)].
(A) 5.0  (B) 6.0  (C) 7.0  (D) 8.0  (E) 9.0
21. A collections agent calls debtors; each call independently reaches a live person with probability 0.35. Calculate the probability that more than 4 calls are needed to reach a live person.
(A) 0.075  (B) 0.130  (C) 0.179  (D) 0.225  (E) 0.288
22. X~Uniform(0,90). A deductible of 30 applies, so the payment is Y = max(X−30,0). Calculate the ratio Var(Y)/Var(X).
(A) 0.444  (B) 0.519  (C) 0.593  (D) 0.667  (E) 0.741
23. A book of 200 independent policies each has probability 0.04 of a claim in a year. Let T be the total number of claims. Using the normal approximation with continuity correction, calculate P(T ≤ 12).
(A) 0.842  (B) 0.885  (C) 0.921  (D) 0.947  (E) 0.968
24. Among 850 claimants, 190 had a prior claim within the last 2 years. Of those, 60 had a prior claim and filed a disputed claim this year. Overall, 255 claimants filed a disputed claim this year. Calculate the probability a randomly selected claimant filed a disputed claim this year, given they had no prior claim within the last 2 years.
(A) 0.176  (B) 0.230  (C) 0.295  (D) 0.361  (E) 0.409
25. A commercial auto policy has 70% coinsurance above a deductible of 400. Losses (in thousands) are Exponential with mean 5. Calculate the probability that the policy payment on a randomly selected loss exceeds 3 (thousand).
(A) 0.198  (B) 0.276  (C) 0.334  (D) 0.392  (E) 0.451
26. A recruiter interviews candidates one at a time; each independently passes with probability 0.4. Calculate the probability that the 3rd passing candidate is found on exactly the 6th interview.
(A) 0.069  (B) 0.104  (C) 0.138  (D) 0.166  (E) 0.207
27. The number of large claims filed monthly by a mid-sized insurer is Binomial with n=15 and p=0.3. Calculate the mode of the monthly claim count.
(A) 3  (B) 4  (C) 5  (D) 6  (E) 7
28. A reinsurer holds 100 independent treaties, each with annual loss having mean 50,000 and standard deviation 20,000. Calculate the approximate probability that total losses across all 100 treaties exceed 5,150,000.
(A) 0.121  (B) 0.174  (C) 0.227  (D) 0.286  (E) 0.341
29. Losses are Exponential with mean 6. A deductible of 2 applies to each loss, so the insurer's payment is Y=max(X−2,0). Calculate the median value of Y.
(A) 1.39  (B) 1.79  (C) 2.16  (D) 2.58  (E) 3.00
30. A policy has an 0.85 probability of no loss and a 0.15 probability of exactly one loss in a year. If a loss occurs, its amount (no deductible applies) is Exponential with mean 4000. Calculate the 90th percentile of the insurer's annual payout.
(A) 811  (B) 1216  (C) 1622  (D) 2027  (E) 2433

Appendix C — Practice Exam Solutions

Full worked solutions for all 30 questions, each tagged with the manual chapter it draws on so you can jump back for review. Try to solve a question completely before reading its solution.

Solutions

1. [Part II, Ch. 4 — Geometric-shaped recursion]
Show Solution
p(n)=p(0)(0.35)ⁿ. Since probabilities sum to 1: p(0)/(1−0.35)=1 ⟹ p(0)=0.65.
P(N≤2) = 0.65 + 0.65(0.35) + 0.65(0.35)² = 0.957125
P(N>2) = 1 − 0.957125 = 0.0429. Answer: (B)
2. [Part I, Ch. 2 — Inclusion-exclusion]
Show Solution
P(A∪B∪C) = 0.35+0.25+0.40−0.12−0.15−0.10+0.05 = 0.68
P(none) = 1 − 0.68 = 0.32. Answer: (C)
3. [Part II, Ch. 9 — Memoryless shortcut for E[(X−d)⁺]]
Show Solution
For Exponential(mean μ), E[max(X−d,0)] = μ·e−d/μ (a direct consequence of the memoryless property).
E[Payment] = 8·e−3/8 = 8(0.68729) = 5.498
Answer: (C)
4. [Part III, Ch. 2-3 — Conditional distribution & variance]
Show Solution
P(Y=1) = 0.15+0.35+0.15 = 0.65. Conditional probabilities: P(X=0|Y=1)=0.15/0.65, P(X=1|Y=1)=0.35/0.65, P(X=2|Y=1)=0.15/0.65.
E[X|Y=1] = (0.15·0+0.35·1+0.15·2)/0.65 = 0.65/0.65 = 1.000
E[X²|Y=1] = (0.35·1+0.15·4)/0.65 = 0.95/0.65 = 1.4615. Var(X|Y=1) = 1.4615 − 1² = 0.4615. Answer: (C)
5. [Part II, Ch. 5 — Poisson]
Show Solution
P(X≥2) = 1 − e−3.5(1+3.5) = 1 − 4.5e−3.5 = 1 − 4.5(0.030197) = 0.8641
Answer: (D)
6. [Part I, Ch. 6 — Addition & multiplication rules]
Show Solution
"Only 401(k)" = 0.70−0.20 = 0.50; "only IRA" = 0.50−0.20 = 0.30; "both" = 0.20.
0.40(0.50) + 0.60(0.30) + 0.80(0.20) = 0.20+0.18+0.16 = 0.54 = 54%
Answer: (D)
7. [Part I, Ch. 3 — Combinatorics]
Show Solution
P = C(7,2)·C(5,2) / C(12,4) = 21·10/495 = 210/495 = 0.4242
Answer: (D)
8. [Part II, Ch. 4-6 — Discrete vs. continuous uniform variance]
Show Solution
Discrete uniform on 20 integers: Var = (20²−1)/12 = 33.25, SD = 5.7663. Continuous uniform on an interval of length 19: Var = 19²/12 = 30.0833, SD = 5.4849.
Ratio = 5.7663 / 5.4849 = 1.0513
Answer: (C)
9. [Part III, Ch. 3 — Conditional moments]
Show Solution
E[C|·] = 0(.2)+1(.3)+2(.35)+3(.15) = 1.45. E[C²|·] = 0(.2)+1(.3)+4(.35)+9(.15) = 3.05.
Var(C|C+D=3) = 3.05 − 1.45² = 3.05 − 2.1025 = 0.9475
Answer: (C)
10. [Part II, Ch. 6 — Exponential median]
Show Solution
Solve 1−e−m/μ = 0.5 ⟹ m = μ·ln2.
median/mean = ln 2 = 0.6931
Answer: (B)
11. [Part III, Ch. 6 — Variance of a sum of i.i.d. variables]
Show Solution
Var(S) = 8 × 360{,}000 = 2{,}880{,}000
Answer: (C)
12. [Part II, Ch. 6 — Poisson process & Exponential median]
Show Solution
Rate λ = 6/3 = 2 per year, so mean time between breakdowns = 1/2 = 0.5 year.
Median = 0.5 × ln 2 = 0.3466
Answer: (C)
13. [Part I, Ch. 7 — Bayes' Theorem]
Show Solution
P(fraud) = 0.30(0.04)+0.45(0.07)+0.25(0.10) = 0.012+0.0315+0.025 = 0.0685.
P(Central | fraud) = 0.0315 / 0.0685 = 0.4599
Answer: (C)
14. [Part II, Ch. 9 — Limited expected value, Uniform]
Show Solution
E[max(X−d,0)] = ∫d200(x−d)/200 dx = (200−d)²/400
Setting this to 40: (200−d)² = 16{,}000 ⟹ 200−d = 126.49 ⟹ d = 73.51. Answer: (C)
15. [Part III, Ch. 4 — Variance of a linear combination]
Show Solution
Var(3X−2Y) = 9(25)+4(16)+2(3)(−2)(6) = 225+64−72 = 217
Adding the constant 10 does not change the variance. Answer: (C)
16. [Part II, Ch. 4 — Binomial]
Show Solution
X~Binomial(5,0.3).
P(X≥3) = C(5,3)(.3)³(.7)² + C(5,4)(.3)⁴(.7) + C(5,5)(.3)⁵ = 0.1323+0.02835+0.00243 = 0.1631
Answer: (C)
17. [Part III, Ch. 6 — Variance of a linear combination, independent case]
Show Solution
Since X,Y are independent, the covariance term vanishes:
Var(W) = 25(7)+9(12) = 175+108 = 283
Answer: (D)
18. [Part II, Ch. 9 — Limited expected value E[X∧d]]
Show Solution
E[X∧d] = μ(1−e−d/μ) = 12(1−e−5/12) = 12(1−0.65924) = 4.089
Answer: (C)
19. [Part I, Ch. 5-6 — Independence, "exactly one" event]
Show Solution
P(wind, no flood) = 0.15(0.75) = 0.1125. P(no wind, flood) = 0.85(0.25) = 0.2125.
P(exactly one) = 0.1125+0.2125 = 0.325
Answer: (C)
20. [Part III, Ch. 5 — Order statistics, max of a Uniform sample]
Show Solution
For Uniform(0,b) and sample size n: E[X(n)] = b·n/(n+1).
E[max] = 10 × 4/5 = 8.0
Answer: (D)
21. [Part II, Ch. 4 — Geometric survival probability]
Show Solution
"More than 4 calls needed" means the first 4 calls all fail to reach a live person:
P(X>4) = (1−0.35)⁴ = 0.65⁴ = 0.1785
Answer: (C)
22. [Part II, Ch. 8-9 — Variance of a deductible payment, Uniform]
Show Solution
Var(X) = 90²/12 = 675. For Y=max(X−30,0): E[Y] = ∫3090(x−30)/90 dx = 20, E[Y²] = ∫3090(x−30)²/90 dx = 800, so Var(Y) = 800−400 = 400.
Ratio = 400/675 = 0.5926
Answer: (C)
23. [Part III, Ch. 7 — CLT with continuity correction]
Show Solution
E[T]=200(0.04)=8, Var(T)=200(0.04)(0.96)=7.68, SD =2.7713.
P(T≤12) ≈ Φ[(12.5−8)/2.7713] = Φ(1.624) = 0.9474
Answer: (D)
24. [Part I, Ch. 4 — Conditional probability from a contingency table]
Show Solution
No prior claim: 850−190=660. Disputed & no prior: 255−60=195.
P(disputed | no prior) = 195/660 = 0.2955
Answer: (C)
25. [Part II, Ch. 8 — Coinsurance & deductible together]
Show Solution
Payment Y = 0.7·max(X−0.4,0) (units of 1000). Y>3 ⟺ X>0.4+3/0.7 = 4.6857.
P(X>4.6857) = e−4.6857/5 = 0.3918
Answer: (D)
26. [Part II, Ch. 4 — Negative Binomial]
Show Solution
P(X=6) = C(5,2)(0.4)³(0.6)³ = 10(0.064)(0.216) = 0.1382
Answer: (C)
27. [Part II, Ch. 4 — Mode of a Binomial distribution]
Show Solution
The mode of Binomial(n,p) is ⌊(n+1)p⌋ when (n+1)p is not an integer.
⌊(16)(0.3)⌋ = ⌊4.8⌋ = 4
Answer: (B)
28. [Part III, Ch. 7 — CLT for a sum of independent losses]
Show Solution
E[Total] = 100(50{,}000) = 5{,}000{,}000. SD(Total) = √100 × 20{,}000 = 200{,}000.
Z = (5{,}150{,}000−5{,}000{,}000)/200{,}000 = 0.75
P(Total>5.15M) ≈ 1−Φ(0.75) = 1−0.7734 = 0.2266. Answer: (C)
29. [Part II, Ch. 9 — Median of a deductible payment]
Show Solution
P(Y=0)=P(X≤2)=1−e−2/6=0.2835, which is below 0.5, so the median is positive. Solve P(X≤y+2)=0.5:
1−e−(y+2)/6 = 0.5 ⟹ y+2 = 6·ln2 = 4.1589 ⟹ y = 2.1589
Answer: (C)
30. [Part II, Ch. 8 — Percentile of a mixed (point-mass + continuous) payout]
Show Solution
P(Y≤y) = 0.85 + 0.15(1−e−y/4000) for y>0. Setting this to 0.90:
0.15(1−e−y/4000) = 0.05 ⟹ e−y/4000 = 2/3 ⟹ y = 4000·ln(3/2) = 1621.86
Answer: (C)

Score Interpretation

Correct (out of 30) Interpretation
25–30 Exam-ready — do a final review of any missed topics and schedule your sitting.
19–24 Solid foundation — revisit the specific chapters behind your misses, then retake a fresh set of practice problems.
Below 19 Return to Parts I–III in full before attempting another timed practice exam.
